Rich men & Great ----- commonly have many others in dependence on him Rich men may bring others into dependence by monopoly as it is called. a Person may have others in dependence on him by his being alone possessed of some Kind of Commodity providentially [^001-note9]

m so likewise others that are not Rich may bring others -- & even the Rich into a dependence by an agreemt among thems. to act as one in disposing of a particular necessary Commodity that they altogether have taken home to thems. & may improve this Advantage. when imp [^001-note10] when men improve these or any other particular Advantages they have over their Neighbours to induce to yield to him of his substance which otherwise he for his unreasonable gain which otherwise he would not not be willing for it is extortortion

This is Robbery [^001-note11] but not ex properly the sin of Extortion. or a man may have a special advantage -- -- privately to take --- not properly extortion. In Extortion the special advantage is improved to induce him by him that has it to Induce his neighb. hims. to yield or give up to him his substance But yet I would further[^001-note12] observe that if a man person may improve a special advantage he has over his his N. to induce him to yield of his S. to him for his unreasonable Gain & which ma yet may not properly be the sin of Ext . --- The Advantage of his Knowl. & anothers Ignorance This is very sinf. Tho tis found the sin of ov Fraud & overreaching . but not properly extortion. because the Person [^001-note13] by through his Ignorance in orde not the sin & deceit may be very free & willing. -- no there is no Constraint Fraud [-] but not extorting any thing against his will. in order to the sins being Exh. -- a man must Improve the Adv. ------ to induce him to yield ----- when otherwise it in is against his will.

and I would obs. once more that a man may Improve some special Adv - ------ to Induce his neighb. to yield of his substance to him as which otherwise would be against his will & yet not be guilty of Extortion. provided that the substance which he by this [^001-note15] means induces him to yield to him ben't his unreasonable Gain some men through Covetousness are not willing to give a Reasona able Price. This is a sin m that men may be guilty of both in buying & in selling But the main Thing [^001-note17] wherein the difficulty lies of applying this defi nition to particular Cases so as to Know what Individual acts are Extortion & what not lies in this Resolving this Q. viz. what may be is an unreasonable Gain & or when may a Persons in buying & selling be said to make an unreasonable Gain or when th may be the Gain that a Person takes advantage to make of his neighbours in his dealings with him be said to be unreaonable I shall say something [^001-note18] to this Enq. with a special Regard to mens [-] mutual commerce or dealing one with another in buying & selling. & Here in the first 1 Place I would observe in some Instances what are not good Rules to go by in Judging of his Matter. 2. I would Lay down some General Rules by which this matter is to be determined.

[^001-note39] subject The sin of Extortion I come now in the (2) Place to lay down some general Rules By which this matter is to be determined 1 One Rule ever to be attended is that we dont seek our own interest in should not Knowing ly & designedly trade so that our own Benefit only should be promoted by it or so that the Bargain that we make should apparently be only for our profit but not at all to the profit of But to the Loss of Him that [^001-note40] Him we deal with but to his Loss when every any man improves any special advantage he has over his neighbour . as some dependence his neighbour has upon him if a man improve or Power he has over him or fear that his his neighbour has upon him to [-] induce his neighbour so to deal with him in buying or selling that the bargain shall be wholly for for his Gain but his neighbours manifest Loss this is Extortion. But this Rule that has [^001-note41] now been mentioned tho it be a Rule alwaies to be attended . men them & be a good Rule so far as it extends . yet it will is not so extensive as to cut off all Extortion in dealing. It is universally true that a men ought not to deal so with their neighbours that the bargain should be apparently for their own Gain & their neighbours Loss . But is not universally true that a man those Bargains wherein gain which are for the Benefit of both parties are Just. A man may be guilty of Extortion in making a Bargain with his neighb. under necessity . that it may be for his neighbours Benefit to take what He takes [^001-note42] what he offers him at his own Price rather than Go without it & suffer the Inconven Extreme Inconvenience that will come upon him without it . & yet He may be guilty of extortion in dem demanding such a Price of him . so that there are some further Rules must be It may be to a mans Benefit that is sick & ready to die for want a particular Remedy ------------

-----

so that there are some [^001-note43] further Rules necessary in order to cut off all Kinds of external. & theref I would obs.

_____________________________

2d . That another Rule universally to be attended is this viz that we should never deal in such a manner as tends to the injury of publick society . And whenever a Person when men take such Advantages through They have of them by either by the general necessity or otherwise to raise Commodities to such a Price as the tends to the injury of the Publick society That altho it be much for the gain of particular Persons yet the publick society in general suffers by it such gain is un- [^001-note44] reasonable Gain . because it is Contrary to the Gr it is inconsistent with the great & fundamental Laws & Rules that G. has given us to whereby to Regulate our Conduct towards our neighbours . [--] It is contrary to that Great Command that is the sum of all the duties of the second table of the Law of loving our neighbour as our selves 'Tis contrary to the Great End of pu all publick society & that is the publick Good. G. has made man a sociable Creature . in with such a nature & su in such Circumstan ces ces [-] as necessarily leads him to society --- cant live alone and tis appointmt that [^001-note45] men should unite thems one But the End But Gods end in thus --- ordering --- is the is the General Good.

Theref. whenever men in their dealings seek their own private Gains in such a manner & measure as apparently tends to the injury of the publick society then his Gains then He seeks unreasona ble Ga then his Gains are unreasonable Gains mens raising the Price of [^001-note51] Commodities to an an a very Great height is very often much to the Hurt of Publick society in many Respects. when men raise will take particular opp. & advantages which they see to Raise the price of what they have to dispose of much beyond the Price which other Commodities in general do bear -- Tho there be nothing special in Provd. with Relation to that Commodity to affording Just Reason why it should be raised beyond such Proportion . It tends very much to the Hurt of publick society in General to shew [ ] the force to throg throw things [^001-note52] into Confusion it ends to excite & provoke others to also to Raise -- of their Commodities or Labours ---- when other wi to Relieve thems. -- --- when otherwise there . would be no need of it & so by causing sudden Changes in the Prices of what is bought & sold . all things are kept unsettled. many doors are opend for those that are illdisposed to injured others & a Great many una will unavoidably suffer Great injury for in such Cases it will be [^001-note53] impossible to keep all things on a due Balance But there will be so much uncertainty & unsettledness with Regard to the supposed value of things That the most Conscientious Persons will necessarily be greatly at a loss and besides per mens thus through a greedy & Covetous sp. striving to their utmost to strain up the price of ---- --- & so doing what tends to raise the in the End needlessly to raise the Price of Commod. in general. deprec issues in the Great de- [^001-note54] preciating of the publick medium of Trade. & that is publick Loss . The Publick Looses what is thus Lost in the value of the medium of trade & so men from a greedy sp. advance their private Interest on the Great Loss & damage of the publick society

& there b & be not apparent --- beyond its due proportion -- considering the scarcity -- &c - ------- & so no Reason on this or any other account to suppo suppose that the Price it will tend to the Hurt of society for the Common Price to be followed then it may be followed but otherwise not [^001-note57]

### 857.Zech. 8'20-22 (2_47) trans

Jonathan Edwards

Fast for the coming of Xs Kingdom. Feb. Tuesday Feb. 3..1746,7.

Zech. 8. 20, 21, 22. Thus saith the Lord of Hosts It shall come to pass that there shall Come People and the Inhabitants of many Cities and the Inhabitants of one City shall go to another saying let us go speedily to pray before the Lord & to seek the Lord of Hosts . I will go also. yea many People and strong nations shall come to seek the Lord of Hosts in Jerusalem & to pray before the Lord.

_____

In this Chap. we have a Prophecy of a future glorious advancemt of the Church of God wherein it is evident something further is intended than ever was fulfill'd to the nation of the Jews under the [^005-note1] old Testam. or before the coming of the mes m. --- for here are plain Prophecies of such Things as never were fulfill'd under the old Testam. in the two last verses of the Chap

___________________

The Prophecy is very agreable to other Prophecies that we have in the old Testam. of the future glorious state of the Chh. of G. & in under the Reig after the in the days of the Gospel In the 3d v. it is said Thus saith the Lord of Hosts I am returned unto Zion & will dwell in the midst of Jerusalem & Jerusalem shall be calld a City of Truth and the mountain of the L. [^005-note2]

of Hosts the Holy mountain[^005-note3] This is as agreable to what we have in the 60. Chap. of Isai. that illustrious prophecy of the future Glo. Times of the Chh. in v. 14. and They shall call Thee The City of the Lord The Zion of the H. one of Israel and that in v in v. 5.

------- & also v that v. 19. -------------- ------ are agreable to Jer. 4 31. 4. Again I will build Thee & Thou shalt be built o virgin of Israel Thou shalt again be adorned with Thy Tabrets & shall go forth in the dances of them that make merry & v. 13. Then shall the virgin rejoice in the dance both young men & old together for[^005-note4] I will turn their mourning into Joy & will Comfort them & They shall rejoyce from their sorrow. & the Expressions we have in the 8. v.

we I say those Expressions are exactly agreable to many we have in other Prophecies relating . & so are those in th 12 v. & so are those in the 12. v. so far as this Prophecy[^005-note5] may be supposed to have Respect to the Jewish nation it cant to be have Respect to the smiles of Heaven upon them that attended their return out of the Babilonish captivity or any thing else that has yet been fulfilled but must & can have its fulfilmt only in that future calli future calling of the Jews appears by v. 7, & 8

we have no account cant have Respect to the Return from the Babilonish Captiv.[^005-note6] --- already past

nor -- to any additions made after such this Time to the number of Returning Captives

have no account of any of the Peopl People carried captive into the western Parts of the Θ -- & returning from thence ----- ------ if it Respects the Jews theref it must have Respect to -----------

USE. that I shall[^005-note28] make of what has been said at this Time is to Exhort all such as as have here present as have the Interest of Xs Kingd. at Heart to comply with the motion that is now made to use us for such united Prayer as as from some of Gods ministers in a distant Land to such united Prayer as is spoken of in the Text & doc. we have lately had that very proposal spoken of in the Text made to us & in like manner & Circumstances as is foretold there foretold & a a part of the Chh of G.[^005-note29] in a di & the proposal has repeatedly been made to some of us a part of the Chh of G. in a distant part of the Θ have said to us come let us go speedily to pray ---- ----- now will it not become us who are applied to make that Reply in the Text --- I will go also They have informed us what They have agreed to of their desi have informed us of their design and b if with a desire that we would join with Them in it properly & suitably proposed one would Think[^005-note30] we should be exceeding glad of it & should receive it as Joyf. Jonathan Edwards

March 1747. + June 1755 st. Ind. [^007-note1]

1 Cor. 6. 11. And such were some of you but ye are washed but ye are sanctified but ye are justified in the name of the Lord Jesus and by the spirit of our God

___________________

two thing concerning the state of the Xtian Corinthians 1 The state

___________

1. The state They were in before they became Xtians.

_____

2. The state they were in after They became Xtians. wa Their being washed signifies their being delivered from their former wickedness their being sanctified has Respect to the contrary vertues & Grace that [^007-note2] were infused Their being justified. & then there is added a twofold Cause or their dependence on two divine Persons in

_____________________________

2. The Causes of this Change. impli Their dependence of on Each of the Persons of the Trinity signified 1. it im is implied that it is God the F. that washes sanctifies & Justi fies. 2. ob we may Observe the Person through whom this is done. This shews the Grac free Grace. 3. The Person that imme [^007-note3] diate Effects it. This shews their dependence on divine Power

______________________________

3. The manner of Expression denotes the Reality Greatness & wonderfulness of this Change.

Jonathan Edwards

July 1747

Exod 9. 12 ----- 16 T ---- Thus saith the L. G. of the Hebrews let my People go that They may serve me For I will at this Time send all my Plagues upon thine Heart ----

____________

1. The sin of Pharoah viz. His Hardness of H. & obstinacy in Refusing to comply with the word of G. by moses 2. The Consequence[^013-note1] 2. His Punishment Gods sending all his Plagues upon him 3. The Consequences 1 Convincing Him 2. destroying Him 3. manifesting the Greatness of Gods Power & majesty. doc. They that will[^013-note2] not yield to the Power of God word shall be broken by the Power of his Hand

1 when There are these two things by which G. mani that G manifests hims. by & by which his designs are carried on viz his word & his works the words of his mouth & the works of his Hand. & The will of G. must & will surely be fulfilled & his de his name Glorified by one or the other of these By the one G. makes Known his will & proposes it & exhibits motives[^013-note3] to mens Reason to Influence them to [-] as moral Agents to comply with the will of God By the other G. undertakes hims. to fulfil his own will & obtain his own End. and theref. all things must be subdued to G. by one or the other of these. Thus G. will prevail over all mankind in one Respect or the other ov som over some he prevails by the power of his word but others will not yield to this They will not bend before the Powerful word of G. as to such they shall[^013-note4] be broken by the Power of Gods almighty Hand HandsThe destruction of Pharaoh is represent ed as his broken in pieces. ### 867. Ps. 61'2 (7_47) trans

Jonathan Edwards

July. 1747.

____________________________________

Ps. 61. 2. From the End of the Earth will I cry unto Thee when my Heart is overwhelmed: lead me to the Rock that is higher than I. 1. The Circumstances that The Psalmist represents speaks of Himself as being in . 1. At a great distance from G. or far from the Comfortable manifestations of his Presence [-] [ ] & In the Th In the Ends of the Earth. [^014-note1]

2. His Heart [-] is overwhelmed . or ready to be overwhelmed

_____________________________

2. How he seeks a Remedy by Crying to unto God. & seeking that he may be lead to Him as his Help.

_____________________________

3. Here is a Reason implied why he seeks Remedy in this way. viz. G. is a Rock that is higher than Tis G. that he means by this Rock as is manifest

_______________________________

represents the sorrows & mischiefs that he is surrounded with as a Flood that is ready to overwhelm him that will soon [^014-note2] get over his Head if He surmount his stature & get over his Head unless He stands on some thing higher than Himself who so that He may be kept above water .

______________________________

doc Christ is as an high Rock on which Persons may stand above the Floods that other wise would overwhelm Them. 1 What Evils men are liable to be overwhel med by as by a Flood. 2. That X is as an high Rock . above the Floods that is Ready to overwhelm the Chil. of men.

_________

3. in X J. may Persons may therefore be secure from those floods I. what Evils. [^014-note3] 1. by the rage of & malice of Temporal Enemies. The Race of mankind by the Fall is become a Generation of vipers . having lost those Principles of holy Love & Charity that reigned in man in his first state their hearts are naturally full of the seeds & Principles of Enmity . [--] both against G. & against their fellow Creature. They are disposed to fight against G. & [---] are become worse than wild Beasts one to another. How full has this fallen Θ been in all ages of bitter Contentions hatred wrath & bitter Contentions war Bloodshed through the Hatred & [^014-note4] wrath that men have Exercised one towards another. and especially have the godly been from age to age liable . ----

-----------

----- -- This Enmity & Cruelty of temporal Enemies [-] is often like a Raging torrent fierce & rapid torrent threatning to bear down & like a migh a mighty Flood. we read of floods of ungodly men Ps. 18. 4. many Times a very [^014-note5] great multitude. Josh. 24. 22. And Joshua said unto the People ye are witnesses against your selves that you have chosen the Lord to serve Him And they said we are witnesses

____________________________

doc. They that dont take Heed to keep thi their vows wherin they have promised to serve G. what They have said & done will be brought as a witness against Them. to Render 'em inexcuseable & to Condemn 'em out of their [^016-note1] own mouths

__________________________

Joshua in telling the People that they were witnesses against Thems. --- has Reference to a future Time when They should be called to an account , as he ha it after they had there had been a Time of trial whether They had would regard their vows or no & after they had actually violated Them. They were no witnesses against Thems. before they They would not have been witnesses against Thems. if they had faithfully kept their Covenant nor were they witnesses against Thems. before They had violated it [^016-note2] when they consent to the words of Joshua & say we are witnesses They dont consent to it that They are witnesses against Thems. now wh before they had broken their Cov. & while They intended to keep it & supposed they should keep it . There is Reference here to a future Time of Account as there is in the 26 & 27 verses. That is this shall be a monument – to put you in mind – when ye depart --- & to so to make you sensible of Gods Justice when Judgmts [^016-note3] come upon you

when a professing People solemnly enter into Cov. with G. & bind thems. by sacred vows to Him G. the Omniscient & infinitely holy G. observes what Passes . & the transaction is not forgotten but is as much Remember'd as if such a Record was kept of it as there was of Israels vows that we have an account of in the Text of which we are told v. 26. that Joshua wrote -------

There are two sorts of desires that may be so called. viz. the desires of the Human nature & the desires of the new nature

1 The desires of the Human nature . i. e the desires which necessarily belong to the Human nature such is a desire of Happiness in General.

I. what is to be under stood by Gods Holiness Gods Thus described Gods Holiness is the Excellency is the moral the Excellency of Gods nature whereby his Heart is dis is disposed & delights in posed to every Thing that is morally Good & Excellent & is opposite to Every thing [^019-note2] all moral Evil.

There are two Kinds of Good & Evil moral & natural. mora By moral Evil is meant the Evil of sin or vice. the Heart the wills or Hearts of Intelligent agents or of their voluntary Actions & so by moral Good in other words moral Evil is the Evil Quality the will or Heart & its acts & Exercises natural Evil so by

according to our way of conceiving of G. it lies in is a disposition or Inclination of the Heart of G. Tho it Tho it must be observed that there is indeed no diff. between Habit & Act . disposition & Exercise in G.

Eccles. 8. 8. There is no man hath Power of over the spirit to retain the sp. neither hath He Power in the day of death & there is no discharge in that war neither shall wickedness deliver Those that are given to it

___________

The wise man in these words sets forth to us the certainty & inevita bleness of death. 1. we may Observe a twofold [--] Representation of this. 1 Tis Represented by the impossibility of retaining the sp. or Keeping that from foresaking the Body of that [^025-note2] Here 'tis Intimated & im- -plied as tis in several other Passages in this Book that the soul dont die with the Body. But departs from it into another state

________________________

2. The impossibility of avoiding death is represented by the compared to a being necessitated to Engage an Enemy in the day of Battle. However terrible the Enemy Enemy may appear & how much soever the Battle may be dreaded. yet there is no discharge or as it is in the margin there is no Casting off weapons . The General of the armny will not acquit any one soldier [^025-note3] in the army [-] Let him be never so much afraid . He must en gage with the Rest

______________

2. – The Time when this will be inevitable -- The day of death appointed Time . The This is The wise man else where observes that in 1 Chap of this Book. 1, 2d v that for every Thing there is a season & a Time a Time to be born & a Time of die . [^025-note4] The latter of these is what is Here here calls the day of death. The Time appointed The Time appointed by Gods decree for any Purpose is often ca in SS. called the day of such an affair. day of vengeance day of wrath day of Grace. day of visitation very f eloquently thus [^025-note5] expressed. day Time & exact limit of day & night are fixed . The sun never fails.

_________________________

3. A particular Case is here mentioned . where death death is spoken of as inevitable in a particular Case in which it is particularly dreadfully . viz when it finds men in wicked ness or g of sin or as it is express given to wicked ness [^025-note6]

Jonathan Edwards

prepared for Mr. Brainerds Funeral appointed Octob. 12. 1747.

-----------------------

_____________

The Apostle here in a discourse continued through the . 3, 4, 5 & 6 Chap of this Apostle speaks [MS sic; "Epistle"] of his own ministry in the Gospel of X insisting on those Things that tends tended to recommend it to the Acceptance of those that He ministred and particularly to the Christian Corinthians that G he had a spiritual Father to & with Regard to whom He had exercised an [^026-note1] Holy Jealousy at this Time some false Teacher or Teachers having broke in among them in his absence & corrupted the minds of main many & had brought the Church of Corinth into great Confusion & brought into a state of iminent danger of yet great Calami ties . It seems that those false Teachers in order to draw off the Corinthians from the doct Gospel that the Apostle had taught Them did spared not to Reproach[-] Him & did industriously & artfully used all & propag by endeavour by [^026-note2] many all meaning to excite & fix in their minds the greatest Prejudices against Him And among other Calumnies which they made us the of against the Apostle They sugg suggested that They sometimes reproachd the doctrine that he Preaches & sometimes his Conduct his Behaviour in the extraordinary Labours & sufferings he went through in performing his ministry. & therefore the Apostle in the 3. Chap Commends the Gospel that He preached. – [^026-note3]

_____

They some & sometimes those fals teachers it seems reproach'd the Apostles Conduct in the Extraordinary Labours & sufferings as tho this was all only a Piece of Craft & Guile in the End to Advance hims. & make a gain of those he Preached to . from these He vindicates Hims. in the which He has Reference to Chap. 4. 2.

moses Priviledge But And here as a special [^026-note50] Enforcemt of this Exh. I would improve that dispensation of Gods Holy Providence that is the sorrowful Occasion of our coming together at this This . viz the death of that eminent servt of Jesus X in the work of the ministry whose Funeral is this day to be attended together with what was observable in Him living & dying

_________

In this dispensation of Providence God shews us our mortality & forewarns us that the Time is approaching when we must be absent from the body & must appear (as the apostle observes in the next v. but [^026-note51] one to the Text) before the Judgmt seat of X that every one of us may receive the Things done in the Body according to that we have done whether it be good or bad. And in him whose death we are called now to consider & improve we hant only an instance of mortality . But as there is the greatest is all imaginable Reason to conclude an instance of one that being absent from the Body is present with the L. whether we Consider the manner of his Conversion . his Experiences the nature of the operation & man he was under & the manner of his Experi ences about the Time whence he dates his Conversion . or the nat his sp. the nature[^026-note52] or manner of the or the nature & Course of th his Experience from Time & his inward Exercises from that Time forward & or his outward Conversation and behaviour & Practice or his Frame & Behaviour during the whole of that long space wherin he looked death in the Face. His Convictions of sin preceeding his first Consolotions in X . (as appears by a written account he has His Convic--------------- Left of his inwards Exercises & Experiences) were exceeding solid deep & thorough but solid His Exercise of mind of mind very great & long-continued but yet sound & solid Consisting in no unsteady & violent Pangs & Hurries unaccountable Frights [^026-note53] Frights & strange perturbations of mind . But arising from the most serious Consideration & proper Illumination of the Conscience . to discern & Consideration the true state of things

___________

And the Light Let into his mind at Conversion & his impressions & Exercises at that Time appear most Clearagreable to Reason & the Gospel of Jesus. The Change very remarkable without any appearance of strang impulsions on the Imagination or perturbations sudden Flights & Pangs of affection & vehement p petu perturbations of animal nature [^026-note54] But attended proper intellectual views of the supream Glory & of the divine consisting in the excellency of the Perfections of his nature . & of the Excellency of the way of salvation by X. This was about 8 years ago when he was 21 years of age That Change which he looked upon as his Conversion was not only a Great Change of the present views affections & Frame of his mind but was evidently been the beginning of Gods work on his Hets Heart which continued ally very sensibly to be carried on from that time forwqard to his dying day . and [^026-note55] [-] was th But the beginning of his work own work . a the Change in which he began to walk in the newness of Life maintaining the vigour of Religiin a daily on holy spiritual walk & with G. in the Inward Ex[er]cises of mind his mind which he kept a Record of of so long as he was able to write & indite till he was no longer able to write or indite but a few days bebefore his death . & as his Conversion was but the Beginning of a Change new Course of inward Experiences & exercises so it was also of a new outward walk & Practice . He greatly abhord the Practice of such as live on their first work & as tho they had now got through their work. thencefound forward by [^026-note56] degrees settled in a as cold Lifeless slothfu negligent Θly frame & had an ill opinion of such Persons Religion. His Talents that his Lord had Comm given Him were in many Respects great His natural Abilities were very singular . very few in this Respect were his Equal. He was of an uncommonly sprightly ready Invention sprightly Apprehension & quick discerning & yet of & very strong memory & yet of a very penetrating Genius Close & Clear Thought & piercing Judgment . He had a great Talent at disputing. clearing up & defending [^026-note57] Truth & confu detect confuting Errour. His Learning was very Considerable He had a great Taste for Learning & applied Hims. with such diligence to his studies when he was in Colledge that he much wounded his Health & was obliged to to th Leave the Colledge & throw by his studies an that account for a season was esteemed one that excelled in Learning when in College . He had a great insight into human nature & an extraordinary knowledge of men as well as things & excelled most that ever I knew in a communicative Faculty He [^026-note58]

He had a peculiar Faculty of accommodating Hims. to the Capacities dispositions & Circumstances of those that he conversed with & would communicate any Instruct or counsel. He had a natural Eloquence & Aptness & Clearness of Expression He had extraordinary Gifts for the Pulpit I never had opportunity to hear Him preach. but often have often heard him Pray & I think his manner of addressing Hims. to G. & expressing hims. before him in that duty inimitable such I so far as I may [--] Judge I have very rarely known equalld He expressed Hims. [^026-note59] with that solemnity propri exact propriety & that solemnity Reverence in such signi ficant Comprehensive pungent Expressions & also with that Reverence devout Reverence & solemnity & Great distance from all affection as forgetting the Presence of men & as being in the immediate Presence of an holy G. that I have seldom Known equalled. rarely Known a paralleld

________________________

& his G manner of Preaching by what I have often heard of it from Good Jud Judges was no less excellent being very clear & Instruc- [^026-note60] tive, natural nervous & affectionate convincing & very convin searching & Convincing . He nauseated a violent noisiness & boisterousness in the Pulpit . & a forced vehemence & all affecta affected vehemence & yet much disrelished a flat cold delivery when the subject & matter of Preach of discourse & matter delivered ma required affection & Earnest- * next Col. ness He often spoke

__________________________

Not only had he excellent Talents for the Pulpit study & the Pulpit but also conversation He was of a sociable Temp disposition & wa was remarkably [col. 2] free & entertaining as well as profitable in his Conver sation ordinary discourse

_____ ________

a Faculty of disputing

_____________________________

* last Col] as he was of a penetrating Judgmt in things in general so especially in matters Relating to experimental. had In this I know I have the concuring Judgment . of some that have long had the name a name for Persons of the best Judgmt . And According to what abi ability I have to Judge of things of this nature & according to my opportunities which of late have been very great I never knw his equal of his & standing for Clear & accu- [^026-note61] rate notions of true Relig. the nature & Essense of true Religion & its distinctions from its various false appearances which I suppose to be owing to these three things meeting together the strength of hisnatural Genius & his own Extraordinary Experiences Great Experience & the great opportunities he had of observation of others in various parts both white People & Indi ans & his own great Experience . not only were his exp his ex

_______________

His Experiences of the Inf d holy Influences of Gods sp. were not great not only at this first Conversion but in the ordinary[^026-note62] Course of his Life from that Time to his dying day. & also very diverse from many Things Experiences that have lately gained the Reputation of high & Eminent Religion the very height of Xtian Experience

_____________

about the Time that this false Religion that mak began to make a very great appearance in the Land. He was for a little while deceived with so as to think highly of it & tho he Knew he never had such Experiences as others told of he thought it was because he others attainments were higher than his & so coveted [^026-note63] 'em & earnestly sought after em but could never obtain them He has told me that he never had what is called an Impulse or an Impression on his Imagi nation in his Life tho while had a good opinion of such things & never could obtain them but ownd that while he that during the short time he thought well of those things he was tinged with that spirit of false zeal [-] that is wont to attend Those that have it but said that even in this time he was not in his Element but was as a fish out of water & that when within two or three months he came to Him self & saw clearly the [^026-note64] E vanity & perniciousness of such Things that it cost him more terrible dist sorrow & Exercise of mind than ever he suffered under first Convic tions the before his first Comfort tho they were very great . & for the Errour that he & for the misstakes in conduct that he ran into during the continuance of that false imprudent zeal He sp shunned not afterwards deeply & openly to humble Hims. to Them that he had offended

________________

And since his Conviction of his Errour He in the forementioned Respects He had ever had a pecu liar Abhorrence of that Kind of false zeal that [^026-note65] & bitter zeal that he for a short time was tinged with & those delusive Experiences that he was deluded into a Good opinion for a short time into a good opinion of

____________

He abhor'd Enthusiasm in all the Forms & op operations of it . He & detested whatever in Principle opinion or experience seemed to verge verge towards Antinomianism. as of those as the Experiences of those whose first Faith consists in believing that X died for them in particular & their first Love in Loving G. because he loves them. or their assurances of their Good Estate from immediate In Testimony or suggestions [^026-note66] that the either with or without Texts of SS. that their sins are forgiven that G. loves Them &c -- & their Joys of such as rejoyced more in their own suposed distinction in pri Honour & priviledges & high Experiences than in Gods Excellency & Xs Beuty & the notions of such as are & the sp. Pride of such Lay men that are setting Thems. up as te publick teachers & cry down the mi huLearning & a Learned ministry & are for & often declared his dislike of & greatly nauseated much noi a disposition in persons to much noise & shew in Religion & being abundant in publishing their own Experience . & the [^026-note67] Experience & sp. of all His own Experiences & spirit were exceeding diverse from these things such Persons who abounded pretended to great discoveries & Joys & went made a great noise about their Experience th But were not engaged in their spirits & careful & diligent i in & Earnest in had their Lives & Practice to glor that they might live to the Glory of G.

________________

He greatly abhord the sp. & Practice of the generality of the separatists His own Experiences were quite of another Kind He never was more full in condeming these things than in his Last illness & after he had despaired [^026-note68] of Life & from time & when he had the clearest views of Et. & the greatest sense of divine things on his Heart & from time to time when he thought hims. actually dying as he Hims. has often said at such Times as he often said he had in a peculiar manner a sense of the dredful & pernicious consequences of this C false Religion & the sp. & Practice that is wont to attend it. & expressed it several times as what would above almost any Thing tempt him to be willing to live that hemight bear testimony against those [^026-note69] Things His own Experiences were quote of another Kind . His Joys as he declared consisted more in actions in doing some thing for God in serving & Glorifying him in the Θ and agreable to this was his Constant Course of Life He not only professed but acted like one whose whole soul . Joy & Happiness it was to glorify G. & as one that had indeed sold all for Christ & who was truly given up to the service of God : He was not one that was for contriving ways to get to Heaven without bearing [^026-note70] t Ease & sloth & without bearing the Cross He was lively in Relig. in the Right way

____________________

He was an instance of one that lived a life for a Life of self-denial was perhaps scarcely to be parallel'd in the English British America in this Age . whoever reads his Publ Journal that has been published may easily perceive that he has lived as one that has served G. with all his might & has greatly denied Hims. in his service But none only by reading that will be overc sensible in any measure to what degree he has denied hims. the Comforts of common Comforts of Life & what Hard things he has undergone[^026-note71] gone through for the advancemt of the K. of X th & the good of the souls of his fellow creatures he has purp purposely concealed those things in his publick Journals which I have learned by long & intimate conversa tion with Him.

____________

His Liberality also has been very in freely spending his own substance & disbursing his own money for the Benefit of others has been very In the course of his unusual. ministry he remarkably imitated the apostle Pauls example. He acted like one that is running a Race in three contending for some great Prize.

___________________________________

In these his Labours & sufferings he Kept his Eye on his Last End. & as he decl [^026-note72] & on his own Heart & as he declares made no account of never made any account of any thing that he did that [-] was not done with an Eye to Gods Glory as his Last End .

______

** & as he acted for G. so he wa acted with a constant dependence his manner in every thing of importance that he undertook was to wrestle with G. for his assistance & blessing. & frequently sought those Things of G. by private secret fasting & Prayer . which he declared to some that were Candidates for the ministry in that stood by Him as he lay on his death bed [^026-note73] as necessary [ ] & declaring the great Benefit he had found by this Practice. [---------] (which he said he never mentioned before) strongly recommending this this Practice to them. And as his desires & Labours for Gods Glory were great & his dependence so great on him so G. was pleased to give him the so was his sucess G. was pleased [-] to make hims. the Instrument of the most wonderful thing of bringing to pass the most remarkable & wonderful Things amongst the poor Brutish barbarous Indians in enlightning awakening & Reforming [^026-note74] them & encouraging[?] them than perhaps any Instance can be produced of in these latter ages of the Θ of which an account is published in his Jour given in his Journals that are published which I would recommend to all such as mi in worth would delight in the take pleasures in the wonders of Gods Grace & would read that will peculiarly tend both to Entertain & profit a Xtian mind [-]

_________

He sometime used that Expression [^026-note75]

an agreable to his Life and as was his Life so was his death and as his Relig. bore him thro carried him through such good Labours & Har self denial & hardship in doing the will of G. in Life so it no less remarkably supported him [-] in suffering the will of G. under the long continued afflictions & distressing of a Languishing & consuming body and a distemper that gradu aly pre from day to day preyed upon his vitals considering how long he looked death in the Face without the least expectation of Life & how often he looked upon Hims. in a dying & was thought by others very near his End his unmoved & [^026-note76] his Constant & unmoved Calmness and Qui Peace of mind & willing ness to submi die may be looked upon no less extraordinary than those things that have been mentioned of Him . He declared on a certain occasi special occ very special occasion wherein the Interest of Relig. seemed to require to speak freely that once when he expected to di his soul would leave his body any minute & had the very clear exercise of his Reason he was had not the least doubt of his Good nor the least fears of death. but added that he should not have so fully declared it had he no thought the Interest of Relig. Required it

_________________

This seemed to be the Case with him continually without without interruption[^026-note77] even to the last The thoughts of approaching death never seemed to damp but rather to encourage him & be pleasing to him what seem'd to damp was the thoughts of deaths being delayed & his being long uns living unserviceable

__________________

The what he made Him especially willing to die & long for it he often declared was that he might glorify G. in Perfection . He often spoke of the day of th his death

______________________

He often used the Epithet glorious when He spoke of the day of the his death & he called it that Glorious day. And the nearer death approached the more desirous he seemed [^026-note78] to be of it

___

He said not long before his death that the Consi deration of the day of death & the day of Judgmt had a long time been peculiarly sweet to him

_____

& said at another Time He could not but Think of the meetness there was in throwing such a rotten carcass as his into the Grave it seemed to Him to be the right way of disposing of it The sabbath before last (sept. 27) feeling an unusual violent appetite to Food & Looking on it as a sign that he was near his End said he should look upon it as a Favour if that might he his dying day. & that He longed for the Time. I am [^026-note79] very willing said He to part with all

_____________

& never he had before expressed Hims. desirous of seeing his Brother again whose return had been expected from the Jerseys but then said I am willing to Go & never to see him again . I care not what I part with to be forever with the Lord

______________

Being asked that morning how he did He answered I am almost in Et. God Knows I long to be there my work is done I have done with all my Friends all the world is nothing to me on the Evening of the next [^026-note80] day. when He thought Hims. a dying & was thought to be dying apprehended to be so by others & he could utter Hims. only by a low broken whispers He often repeated the word Et. & said I shall soon be with the holy angels – He will come He will not tarry

_____

said to me one night as he went to Bed He expected to die that night & he was not at all afraid H I am willing says He to go this night if it be the will of G. death is what I long for He sometimes used that Expression O why is his [^026-note81] Chariot so long a coming & sometimes expressed Hims. as willing to die that minute if it was the will of G. as having nothing to do but to die and willing to go that minute if it was the will of G.

__

He once told me that He had longed for the outpouring of the sp. of God & the glorious Times of the Chh & hoped that they were coming & should have been willing to have lived to promote Relig. at that Time if that had been the will of G. & says he I am willing it should be as it is I would not have the Choice to make my self for 10000 Θs [^026-note82] He once said I am so ragged & polluted that it is difficult for me to conceive of such a creatures going to Heaven But the perfect Righ. of X ! O that Righ. is sufficient . --- These filthy Garments shall be

_______

Tis meet to me to think of Et. the End rent from me closeness of it makes it sweet

---------

_______________

He several Times spoke of the different ways of being willing to die & spoke of an Ignoble way of willingness to die to be willing being willing to die to only to get rid of Pain or to go to Heaven fo only for to get Honour & advancemt there His longing to die appeared evidently to be a quite of another Kind as appeared by such Expressions as what he uttered from [^026-note83] time to time . when He was first taken with some thing like a diarhea diarrhea which is looked upon as one of the Last sypt & most fatal symptoms in a Consumption . He said O the Glo. ti O now the Glo. time is coming I have longed to serve G. Perfectly & G. will gratify these desires

________

& at one time & another He uttered such Expressions as these . my Heaven is to please G. & & glorify Him & give all to Him & to be wholly devoted to his glory . that is the Heaven I long for . That is my Relig. & that is my Happiness & alwaies was ever since ever [^026-note84] since I supposed I had any true Relig. & all those that are of that Relig. shall meet me in Heaven . I dont Go to Heaven to be advanced but to give honour to G. tis no matter where I shall be stationed in Heaven whether I have a high seat or or low seat there but to love & please & Glorify G. . If I had a Thousand souls if they were worth any Thing I would give em all to God . But I have nothing to give when all is done It is impossible for [^026-note85] any rational Crea. to be happy without acting all for G. God hims. could not make me happy any other way I am not an Angel but I long to serve G. as perfectly as the Angels I long to be in Heaven praising & glorifying G. with the H. Angels . all my desire is to glorify G. my Heart goes out to the burying Place it seems to me a desirable place . But Oh to glorify G. that is it that is above all Tis a great Comfort to me to think that I have done a little for God in [^026-note86] the Θ tis but a very small matter yet I have done a little . & I lament it that I have not done more for him . There is nothing in the Θ worth living for but doing Good & finishing Gods work doing the work that X did . I see nothing else in the Θ that can yield any satisfaction besides living to G. pleasing him & doing his whole will next col. † [^026-note87] as his Brother once came along with a Bible in his Hand he said O that lovely Book that dear Book I shall soon see it opend The mysteries that are in it & the mysteries of divine Provid. unfolded *

_______________________

my Greatest Joy & Comfort has been to do something for promoting the Interest of particular Persons & the Relig. & the Good of the souls of particular persons

___________________________

† [^026-note88]

* He was after he came to be in so low a state that He ceased to have the least Expectation of Life his mind was peculiarly carried forth with earnest concern for the Prosperity of the Chh on of G. on Earth & longing desires that Religion might flourish in this Θ which seem'd most very mani festly to arise from a pure disinterested Love to X & concern for his Glory. it could not be from any It is The Prosperity of Zion was a Theme that he dwelt much upon in his C & spoke much of of & more & more the [^026-note89] nearer death approached. He told me that He never had his Thoughts & mind so led forth in all his Life is so led forth in Longi desires & Earnest Prayers for the Prospe Flourishing of Xs Kingd on Earth as since He was so exceeding brought so exceeding low at Boston . He seemed much to wonder that there appeared no more of a disposition in ministers & People to pray for the flourishing of Relig. through the Θ a little before his death he said to me as I came

into the Room . my Thoughts said He have been employed on the old dear Theme the Prosperity[^026-note90] of Gods Chh on Earth I as I waked out of sleep says He I was led to Cry for the pouring out of Gods sp. & the flourishing of XKingd. which the dear Redeemer did & suffered so much to L for tis that especially makes me long for it . But a few days before his death He desired us to sing a Psalm that was concerning the Prosperity of Zion which He signified that his Heart was upon above all Things

_____

His own Congregation especially Lay much on his Heart . He often spoke of Them on his death & commonly when he did so His speech was drowned [^026-note91] with weeping interrupted & drowned with weeping Thus I have imperfectly endeavoured to represent something of the Character & Behaviour of that Excellent servant of X whose funeral is now to be attended tho I have done it very imperfectly yet I have endeavoured to do it faith fully & as in the Presence [-] & fear of G. without Flattering which [ ] is to be abhored in ministers of the Gospel when speaking as messengers of the L. of Hosts. [^026-note92] such Reason have we to believe satisfied in it that the Person we that has been spoken of is now he is absent from the Body is present with the Lordd & not only so but also with Him [^026-note93] now wears a Crown of Glory of distinguished Lustre & Brightness And how much is there in the Consideration of such an Example. & so blessed an End to stir us all up diligent Excite us who are yet alive to with the greatest diligence & Earnestness to improve the Time of Life that that we also may [^026-note94] go to be with X when we forsake the Body The time is coming & will soon come & we know not how soon when we must be also Lay down these Earthly Tabernacles . O how well is it worth the while to give diligence to Lay up in state a good Founda. for our of support & supply against that Time . How How much is such a Peace of mind on a death bed worth . & how dismal would it be to be in such Circumstances as He be under the outward distresses of a Consuming dissolving Frame & Looking [^026-note95] death in the Face from day to day . with sin unforgiven Hearts uncleansed & sin unforgiven under a dreadful Load of Guilt & divine wrath having much sorrow & wrath in our sickness & nothing before us to Comfort us or support our minds nothing bebefore us but an Angels In almighty infinitely Holy An an awful an appearance before an appearance before the Judgmt seat of an [^026-note96] almighty infinitely Holy . angry G. & Endless Eternity inflicting his wrath without mercy . The Person we have been speaking of had a great sense of this He said on not [-] long before his death . Tis sweet to me to think of Et. the Endlessness of it makes it sweet but oh . what shall I say to the Et. of the wicked I cant mention it nor think of it the [^026-note97] Thought is too dredfull at another Time speaking of an Heart devoted to G. & his Glory He said O of what importance is it to have such a Frame of mind such a Heart as this when we come to die Tis that new that gives me Peace

______

How much is there in particular in the things that have been observed of this minister of J. X . to excite us who are called to the same great work of the Gospel ministry. to earnest Care & Endeavours that we may be in like manner faithfull in [^026-note98] our Great work. that we may be filled with the same sp. animated with the same like Great Love to G. & earnest concern to advance the Glory of Christ & the Prosperi ty of Zion . How amiable did those Principles render this servant of X in his Life & how blessed his End. He in a ver remarkable degree followed the Example of the Blessed apostle & when he in the work of the ministry & theref when he came to die He had the like peace that the apostle had who said in the approaches [^026-note99] of death I have fought g a good fight --

_____

Fasting & Prayer see back ________ **[^026-note100] we also must s The Time will soon come when we also must Lay down these Earthly Tabernacles & must go to our Lord that sent us to to Labour in his Harvest to Render an account of our selves to us him H O how there does it concerns us so to run to Run not as uncertainly so to fight not as those that beat the air & to take And should not what we have heard excite us us to come to follow [^026-note101] the Example that has been Now set before us of depend com dependence on G. for his Help in Assistance in our Great work & of frequent secret Fasting & Prayer seeking much of the Influences of his sp. by Fasting & Prayer He expr on his deathbed was often speaking of the great need ministers have of mcuh of the sp. of X in their especially work. & wi in discoursing with some young men that were intended for the ministry & how little good they were like to do without it . How the sp. & How when ministers were under the Influences [^026-note102] of the sp. of G. it assisted them to come at the Consciences of men and as he expressed it as it were to handle them with Hands where as with the sp. of G. whatever elo Reason & Eloquence they might use They did only as it were make use of stumps instead of Hands

_____

O that The Things that were seen in his the Extraordinary example of in Life. his Holi ness Heavenliness Laboriousness & selfdenial in Life & devoting all these Glory of G. & the remark[^026-note103] & the extraordinary Frame of mind he manifested in the so steadfast a manner in the Expectations of death & under the Pains throught that brought it on effectually stir us all excite in us all up both ministers & People a sense of the Greatness of the work we have to do in the Θ the Excellency & and amiableness of thorough Relig. in Heart & Practice & the Blessedness of the End of such whose deaith con finishes such a Life & the [^026-note104] Infinite value of their future Reward when absent from the Body & present with the Lord. & effectually stir up that in the way of such an Life we holy Life we may at last come to so blessed an End . answ. [-] I would Obs. that we know not particularly But Thus far we may determine that it will be in doing Good one to another It certainly will be in doing Good. not in doing Good to G. -- profiting Him. [^027-note10]

it will be in deeds of Love Love is the fulfillmt of all Righ. Love either Love to G. or Love to fellow Crea. Their Love to G. can be directly & immediately is in worshipping & pr acts of worship praise & no other way but by acts of Love one to another by doing Good. [^027-note11]

not in doing Good to Reprobate Creatures an dev Reprobate angels. men theref.

service of G. in H. d in this some things differs from the service of G. on Earth But the difference is not in this that it dont consist in serving fellow Crea. & doing Good to them Bu The diff. seems to lie in the following things not in doing Good to their outw. man. need no supplies. not in acts of [^027-note15] mercy. not attended with sufferings or selfdenial not attended with Labour

Promoting the one anothers sp. Happiness

tho perfect. Capable of addition & Improvemt angels angels make Improvemt [^027-note16] & Advances. in Knowledge Instrumts of prom assist one another in their Contemplations p Instrumts of promoting one anothers Improvemt in Knowl. in Love. ex Each others Exercises of Love. may be in many ways de Conversing declaring . 1 The Golden Candlestick this is not expressly mentioned in the words Read but is plainly Refered to as that whih th Receives the oil emptied out of the Golden Pipes & is expressly mentioned in the verse immediately preceding Prophets Ques Enquiry in the verse immediately preceding the Text. of which the first Question in the first v. of the Text is a Repetition in words a little diverse Then answered I. ------

we have a description of this Candlestick. v. 2.

The Thing Represented[^028-note2] by this Candlestick is most plainly & Evidently the Chh of G.

The Chh of G. is said by the apostle to be the pillar & Ground of the Truth 1 Tim. 3. 15 & the same is Represented by X as a Candlestick with[^028-note3] a Light shining upon it Matt. 5. 14, 15, 16 & the The seven Chhes of Asia are Represented by seven Golden Candlesticks Rev. 1. 20 & tis the whole drift & design of the Revelation made to made to the Proph. Zech. in this Chap. shew that --- 2. The another[^028-note4] symbol or Emblem here exhibited is the Golden oil . coming into the Candlestick. to make it burn & shine . This it is very plain signifies the Grace of Gods sp. oil is abundantly made use of in SS. as a type to Represent the Holy & sanctifying Communications of the sp. of G. Tis apparent the Holy anointing oil was to signify. Isai. 61. 1. The account we have of[^028-note5] the Fulfilmt of this in J. X shews thisLuke. 4. 18. Grace is spoken of ex as being like the holy anointing oil.Ps. 133. 1, 2. The Grace of the H. sp. is called an anointing. 1 Joh. 2. 20 ------------------ 27 ------------------- oi The oil in the Parable of the ten virgins . [^028-note6] that fed the Lamps of the wise virgins si evidently signifies Grace in the H.

FIRST observe the following I Take notice of several Things that we taught in the Text concerning the Things that we are here taught ought to be found in mini those officers that are called to be ministers of the sp Good of the[^028-note18] Chh.

________________

I. They ought to be truly gracious & vitally united to X . Here They are represented as Branches of the good olive Tree in X whose Root & stock is J. X the good olive Tree & not dead dry Brown Branches but living Branches that do indeed derive sap from from the stock the Roo stock

______________

or oil from the stoc They are olive Branches that yi yield oil. But the Branches of the olive tree yield any oil that ben't those that are living Branches the oil that The Fruit that they bear & the oil that They yield is all by sap & vital Influence with which They are replenished[^028-note19] from the Root stock. in vain would golden Pipes be inserted into dead branches in order to Convey any oil from Them to the Candle stick There being called anointed one implies that They are gracious ones The Holy anointing oil of old in the Tabernacle & Temple with its sweet perfumes & excellent Fragrancy as has been already observed represented the Grace of Gods sp. that ointment doubtless a spiritual anointing is her intended which when it is said They are the anointed ones doubtless a sp. anointing[^028-note20] is intended when it is said They are anointed ones . but what a sp. anointing is the apostle tells we learn by the apostle John. 1 Joh. 3. 20. ye have an unction from the Holy one & ye know all Things v. 27. But the anointing which ye have received of Him abideth in you & ye need not that any man should teach you but as the same anointing teacheth you all things & is truth & is no lie.

_____

The particular Graces of Gods sp. are abundantly compared in the Book of Solomon song to those spices that were made use of to preserve[^028-note21] the Holy anointing oil. That holy anointing oil was called the [-] emphatically the precious ointmt. & that Expression in the Text the Golden oil is as of the same import tis as much as to say most precious oil. II. It[^028-note22] is signified in the Text that ministers of X ought to be eminently gracious & near to X. These are spoken of as a anointed ones eminently anointed ones by way of anointed ones emphatically all the saints [^028-note23] an an unction from the Holy one & so are anointed ones but these are the[^028-note24] anointed ones . so spoken of by way of eminently as as it is in the Hebrew These are sons The sons of oil. The so is the [-] so are the words in the Hebrew these are the true[^028-note25] sons of oil that stand before the God of the Earth & as by[^028-note26] oil here is meant the Grace of Gods sp. as has been shewn such as expression according to the Hebrew Idiom signifies one those that are eminently Gracious as the a son of Belial signifies one eminently wicked [-] The son of Perdition antiX is called the son of Perdition signifying that is eminently a destroyer & devo by and of God devoted to eminent destruction . These sin sons of oil are Represented as subordinate Fountain of Grace for the supply of his Chh. the stream of a stream of oil for the supply of the Candlestick is out of Them But the Benefit communicated[^028-note27] is first in the Fountain and is there Eminently.

________

Gods saints by the communications of the oil of Grace do shine as Lights in the O Philip. 2. 15. Their Grace Produces Good works causes their Light to shine before men Like a candle or Lamp on a Candlestick. Matt. 5. 15, 16. neither do men light a Candle & put it under a Bushel but on a Candlestick & it giveth Light to all ------ Let your Light so shine. --------- [-] so in the T our c the tex so in the Text in Reference to the golden Candlestick[^028-note28] spoken of [-] before . that by the oil Communicated had Lamps burning & shining upon it that were Upon it shining & giving Light. But the ministers of X are in SS. represented as Luminaries from whence the Chh of X derives its Light whereby it is enlightened & enabled to shine before men & so are subordinate fountains of Light Light is firstly & eminently in the Luminary & is the They are stars held in the Right Hand of X to give Light to Illuminate the Chhs as the matter is represented in Rev. 1. 16. But Light is first in the Luminary & more eminently[^028-note29] than in those that are enlightened by it them mi The ministers of X by being eminently baptized with the H. Gh. & with Fire should be as is said of J. the Bap. burning & shining Lights. This is most agreable to Reason surely they that are set to be teachers of of others should thems. be be eminently in Knowl. They that set to all saints are united to X X but the tex the Golden Candlestick has a communication with the Olive tree. but But the text [-] represents ministers as eminently near to [^028-note30] Him. Those are the in anointed ones that stand by the God of the Earth

_________________

& are branches of the olive Tree that are a stricter union with the oli Tree than the Golden Candlestick. tho that also has an Communication with it. III. Another Thing taught[^028-note31] us in the words is that ministers should have their entire & continual dependence on X for all Fitness for their work & assistance & success in it . The dependence of ministers on X is livelily Represented by the dependence of the Branch on the stock . The Branch stands on the stock & is inserted into it & grows upon it. yea it grows out of it. it has its very life its bounty Fruitfulness yea & its very being from the stock. every thing appertaining the Branch is from thence The Branch is selfsufficient[^028-note32] in nothing It receives & not only received all from it at first but continually receives all from it it depends on continuall communication from the stock Tree all its sap is derived from thence it flowing into it from thence continually. The Branch dont live by the sap & life Received from the Tree heretofore it cant live by a stock of sap Received in Times past but there must be a present Communication if that be ceases the Bran by the branches being Cut off separated from the Tree th it quickly dies notwithstanding any stock Received in times past The Branch cleaves[^028-note33] Fast to the stock, & from thence is as it were constantly sucking its sap & life. & lives not by its own [-] Life but by the Life of the stock. The Olive Branches in the text cleave to the Tree of which they are Branches & from thence constantly every moment receive oil. the oi golden oil constantly flows into them out of this Tree . & out of Them through the Golden Pipes into the Candle stick Thus ministers ought to Cleave to X their Glorious allsufficient[^028-note34] m Lord & master & the and derives supplies of Knowl Grace & strength from Him by Faith. These olive branches it is said are the sons of oyl that stand by the G. of the Earth by the great Immanuel. The word translated stand also signifies abide. These branches abide by or abide in the God of the Earth The Phrase is parallel with those used by our saviour Joh. 15. 4. abide in me & I in you as the Branch cannot bear Fruit of it self except it abide in the vine no more can[^028-note35] ye except ye abide in me . Christ says these words to his Eleven disciples that he intended should be chief ministers of his Kingdom He exhorts here teaches em the necessity of Cleaving to him abiding in him & Cleaving to him & by Faith & constantly depending on him in order to their bearing Fruit . & by the Fruit that they should bear Fruit here he dont only mean the Fruits tha of holy Practice that they should bear as Xtians but the Fruits of their ministry in the when they should go forth as his messengers to preach the Gospel & convert convert souls to Him[^028-note36] as appears by what follows in the 16 verse. ye have not chosen me but I have Chosen you & ordained you that ye should go & bring forth fruit. That is he had ordained em to be go forth as his messengers into all the world & to be abundantly Fruitful & successful in the work of the ministry as they were after his ascension . The same Fruit that X speaks of his bringing forth . Chap. 12. 23, 24. And J. answered Them saying verily the Hour is come that the son of man should be glorified verily verily I say unto you Except a Corn of wheat fall into the Ground it abideth not & die it abideth alone but if it die[^028-note37] it bringeth forth much Fruit

__________

ministers as I observed before are are to set to be Luminaries to enlighten the Chh. but they have no light of their own but all is derived from X who is the Light of the O & they can be of no use to enlighten the souls of men unless held up by X [-] as a Candle or Lamp can be of no use to direct a Traveller unless held in somebodies therefore they are Represented hand. as stars held in his right Hand Rev. 1. 16. & Chap 2. 1. [^028-note38]

IV. Another thing taught us in the Text is that ministers should be strict & exemplary in their walk & conversation This is one thing include implied in one Thing implied in their standing by the Lord of the Earth [-] the same walking closely with him & that they as this is one thing that they ever Cleave to him in their Practice & dont wicked ly depart from him as this is one Thing [^028-note39] implied in that precept of X to his disciples abide in me. & so as ministers ought to be eminent in Grace so they should be in the Fruits of Grace in a holy Life . & shoul so Ensamples Thus min they should doing that Precept of the apostle Thus they should comply th with the counsel of the apostle Peter. in who exhorts ministers to be ensamples to the Flock . & that of the apostle Paul 1 Tim. 4. 12. Be thou an example of the Believers in word in Conversation in charity in Faith in Purity all Xs disciples by[^028-note40] his direction should be Lights to othes & the way that They are to cause their Lights to shine for others Benefit is by this their Good works which They behold . Matt. 5. But ministers especially are set to be light who are represented as stars held in the Right hand of X are set to be Lights to others They theref. in a peculiar manner should cause their Light to shine bright in this Respect & so should enlighten their Hearers not only by teaching them their very duties & the vertues they should practi exercise but shewing[^028-note41] those things to them giving em a plain instance & bright Example of the thing they teach them It becomes a shepherd not only to lead the Flock . not only to direct the flock by his voice but to Go before em & shew em the way they should go. V Another thing taught[^028-note42] us in the Text is that ministers ought to be communicative of sp. Good. These The olive Branches are Represented not only as constantly receiving the Golden oil from the Tree but Constantly communicating it to the Candlestick . not that ministers are able to bestow this precious Gift of divine Grace to their hearers of Thems. But they are to diligently be diligent & in the use of proper means & Endeavours in order to it . They should as the apostle says didaktikoi[^028-note43] apt to teach Lovers of the souls of me their Hearers . earnestly desiring & Laboriously seeking their sp. Good They should make the Chh. They the & congregation they are set over their Constant Care as those olive Branches these anointed ones are Represented not only as [-] standing by the G. of the Earth but also constantly standing by the Candlestick one on the Right & the other on the [^028-note44] Left con to take care of it continually to supply its Lamps with oil oil that[^028-note45] their Light may never go out through an Intermission of the communication of oil oil. ministers are watchmen the Business of a the Business of a watchman is such as greatly requires Constancy & allows of no intermissions for sleep. Those that G. has set to be watchmen on the walls of Jerusalem should in some Respect be like him that keeps Israel who never slumbers nor sleeps [^028-note46]

The VIth & Last thing I shall observe taught us in the Text is that ministers in their Endeavours for the Good of their Churches & Congregations should use those means that G. the means of Gods appointmt These are[^028-note47] the golden Pipes through which these olive Branches convey Oil into the Can Golden Bowl of the Candlestick. ministers should preach the Gospel of X & not another Gospel instead of it nor should they pervert the Gospel of X They should preach the doctrine of X the pure word of[^028-note48] God. when God sent Jonah when to He was sent to the ninevites was can He said He told Him he should preach the Preaching that He bid him Jonah. 3. 2. ministers should pr not preach of their own word but the word of Christ should communicate to their hearers the pure unadulterated th word of G. & not be as those that corrupt the world word or add their own to it their own notions & vain imaginations or teaching for doctrines the Traditions of men & They should declare the[^028-note49] whole Counsel of G. without dimi adding or diminishing. & should administer the ordinances of X as he has instituted them without mutilating or adding to them or any Human Invention or in any Respect Changing them

______

These are the Golden Pipes these are the most precious & excellent means which infinite wisdom hath provided if we add any thing If we in any Respect Change them vainly by taking any thing from em or adding any thing to em or in any Respect Changing Them thinking to make them better we shall[^028-note50] only pollute them & corrupt them

as G. told his People Israel that if they lifted up their tools upon his altar to hew the stones of it they polluted it Exod. 20. 25.

Tis Gods means & these only that ministers must be used to Heal souls use when Elisha directed naaman to wash in Jordan for the Care of his Leprosie He disliked the means & said that the[-] Rivers of damas cus cus were better than all the waters of Israel but it was the waters of Jordan that Healed him & those only could heal Him. [^028-note51]

I Come now to the SECOND Thing proposed in the doctrinal Handling --- viz to Observe what w the Text teaches of the Happy Fruits & Benefits of those things in the minis ters when found in the ministers of X. two Things 1. APPLICATION[^028-note52] In the Improvemt of what has been said I would in the 1st Place apply the things that have been observed to my & to others my Brethren in the ministry with a & with a more special Regard to Him that is now to be installed in the Pastoral office of the Chh of X in this Place

I. Use may be of Information . Hence we may learn what are the Qualifications that ought to be Regarded in introducing Persons into the ministry. not only that they should be men of Learning and men of orthodoxy & of Civility . but also that They be Persons that there be good Reason for that shall justly weigh[-] with & govern a Reasonable[^028-note53] Charity to induce others to bel believe that They are men of true Piety. vitally indeed sons of oil vitally united to the L. J. X Living Branches in that olive Tree without which there can & those that do truly walk with him & are devoted to him & trust in Him. be able something that shall give em Reasonable satisfaction in this matter for it is a matter of vast Consequence to them

for tho I will be far from saying as some have said that an unconverted minister[^028-note54] never is made the Instrument of Converting souls ------ G. is sovereign unconverted ministers may Preach the word of G. --- -- & ad administer the ord. of G. ----- & G. is sovereign & may ------------ --- But this I will say that it cant be reasonably expected that those who are not in this Respect sons of oil thems. are not indeed branches in the G X [-] are not indeed living Branches in X & sons of oil thems are not likely to be the Instruments of Conveying much of that Golden oil that we read of in the Tetxt from X into the Hearts of men . I say not this that I[^028-note55] think it would be at all proper for every individual Person in a Congrega. to Go to a Candidate They have on probation examine . --- ----- for if they should do it & the minister should submit to these Examina. many of there will be many of such a multitude that are by no means fit Judges in such a Case ----- yea there are many good men . . - very poor Judges . -- by no means sensible of sufficiently studied in the word of G. not thoroughly acquainted with the distinctions the many Counterfeits not only had need to be not[^028-note56] only Persons of Good Expe ---. ________but thorough divines . used in the study

& besides such a Practice as this would naturally Cherish sp. Pride . --- to set all young & old great & small males & female to such work as this. - [--] breed nothing but division ------ nor would it be safe for the Pro ------- & big with Innumerable mischiefs

It is a safer & better way for a People in this case to Jud be governed by the Good Reports that one has obtained & has had established with those Persons of discretion -- & Piety --- that have been best acquainted with[^028-note57] togethe & particularly the Judgmt of those ministers of the Gospel tha whose Business it is to Enquire into & Judge of his Qualifica. ------ & Openly to approve of Candidates for the ministry . --- together with what is visible of the Persons strict & holy Conversa.

X has enquire into & Judge of the Business X has made it to License & ordain ministers[^028-note58] others & to enquire into & Judge of their Qualification ought more immediately to enquire into this matter.

not that I would have ministers act as tho they were searchers of Hearts others Hearts . or who had any Gift of discerning -- --- & can come to any Certainty. --- -- in order to Know whether -- Converted -- They must be able to search the Heart . --- & so they must in order to Know whether --- orthodox in his Judgmt But I see no Reason in the O why ministers in their Exam[^028-note59] nation --- --- ought not to Insist on a serious & credible Profession of those things ---

as much as they should insist they should profess their Judgmt with Regard to the doctrines of Faith

we cant come to a certainty in either of these Cases but we can be under much better advantages to Judge b in [-] both of them by the [ ] a proper Examination & a serious & particular Profession than if the[y] professed nothing & pretended to nothing neither in one Case nor the other [^028-note60]

tis in its own nature as proper and Reasonable that a Person should honour G. by professing Love to him & a supream Love to him & --- & --- -- & does give Hims. up --

as that he sh truly believes such & such doctrines This seems to be peculiarly Reasonable in those that undertake that Great & im most important & sacred Business of a Gospel minister II may be of Exhortation[^028-note61] & under this Use I would apply my self to tw apply what has been ob apply my self to two sorts of Persons. _______________________________1. I would humbly apply what has been observed to my self & to my Brethren in the ministry with a special Regard to him who is now sole to be solemnly introduced to the office of a Pastor of this Chh & Congregation what been Observed from this Part of Gods Holy word shews us what are the Qualifications & Behaviour that we should seek after[^028-note62] & see to That we are living branches in the Good olive tree we ar sons of oil those that have an anointing from above to teach us all things That we be strong in the Grace that is in X Jesus grow in grow strong in the Grace that is in X Jesus. & be under X as it were fountains of Grace . fountains of living waters as X promised to his disciples of old Joh. 7. 38, 39. that we may be burning & shining Lights bur stars [-] in the Right Hand of X full of Light set to enlighten the Chh. of G. & that we have our Entire & Continual dependence[^028-note63] on X for all fitness for our work & all assistance & [--] sucess in it sensible of our own Blindness weakness & unworthiness & that we walk Closely with X & the dont depart from Him that we ensamples to our Flocks of of all Xtian Holiness of every amiable disposition & Practice . & that we be communicative not slothful Earnestly & Laboriously seeking the Good of precious souls being willing to spend & be spent for Them & we in our work use the means of Gods appointmt[^028-note64] & them only. communicating to our hearers the unadulterated milk of the work word & administring the ordinances which X has appointed & as he has appointed Them
